7.2
Video Segmentation from Noisy Frames

An important task in video segmentation is background-foreground separation. There are several matrix factorization algorithms that can achieve decent performance in video segmentation, including robust PCA
(
Candès et al., 2011
)
, PerPCA
(
Shi and Kontar, 2024
)
, and HMF
(
Shi et al., 2023
)
. However, the separation is much more challenging when the videos are corrupted by large noise
(
Shen et al., 2022
)
.
TCMF
can naturally handle such tasks with its power to recover global and local components from highly noisy measurements.

We use a surveillance video from
Vacavant et al. (2013)
as an example. In the video, multiple vehicles drive through the circle. We add large and sparse noise to the frames to simulate the effects of large measurement errors. More specifically, similar to
7.1
, we sample each entry of noise from i.i.d. Bernoulli distribution that is zero with probability
0.99
and nonzero with probability
0.01
. And each entry is sampled from
\{-500,500\}
with equal probability. Then we apply
TCMF
on the noisy frames to recover
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g},\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i)}\}
. We set
\rho
to
0.95
and use
T=15
epochs. The subroutine
JIMF
is still implemented by
HMF
with spectral initialization. We also set the number of iterations for
HMF
to
500
. This is a conservative choice to ensure small optimization error
\epsilon
in the subroutine. To visualize the results, we plot global components
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{T}
and local components
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l}^{T}
. They are shown in Table
2
.

Table 2
:
Foreground Background separation

Frame

1

2

3

Original

noisy

frames

Noise

Global

components

Local

components

In Table
2
, the background and foreground are clearly separated from the noise. The result highlights
TCMF
’s ability to extract features in high-dimensional noisy data.

We compare
TCMF
to several benchmark methods, namely JIVE, HMF, RJIVE, RaJIVE, and Robust PCA. These algorithms, including JIVE, HMF, RJIVE, and RaJIVE, are capable of producing joint and individual components of video frames. In our evaluation, we consider the joint component as the background and the individual component as the foreground. As for robust PCA, we flatten each image into a row vector and create a large matrix
\mathbf{M}_{\text{stack}}
by stacking these row vectors. We then utilize the nonconvex robust PCA
(
Netrapalli et al., 2014
)
to extract the sparse and low-rank components from
\mathbf{M}_{\text{stack}}
. The low-rank component is regarded as the background, while the sparse component captures the foreground.

To assess the performance of these methods, we calculate the differences between the recovered background and foreground compared to the ground truths. Specifically, we estimate the mean squared error (MSE), peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR), and structural similarity index (SSIM) of the recovered foreground and background with respect to the true foreground and background. The comparison results are presented in Table
3
.

In Table
3
, a lower MSE, a higher PSNR, and a higher SSIM signify superior recovery quality. In terms of background recovery, both
TCMF
and robust PCA exhibit low MSE, high PSNR, and high SSIM, surpassing other methods. This suggests that both algorithms effectively reconstruct the background. This outcome was anticipated as
TCMF
and robust PCA possess the capability to differentiate between significant noise and low-rank components. In contrast, other benchmarks either neglect large noise in the model or rely on heuristics. Furthermore,
TCMF
showcases marginally superior performance in MSE and PSNR compared to robust PCA, signifying higher-quality background recovery.

When it comes to foreground recovery,
TCMF
outperforms benchmark algorithms significantly across all metrics. The inability of robust PCA to achieve high-quality foreground recovery is likely due to its inability to separate sparse noise from the foreground. JIVE and HMF yield high MSE and low PSNR, indicating noisy foreground reconstruction. Although heuristic methods, such as RJIVE and RaJIVE, exhibit slight performance improvements over JIVE and HMF, they still fall short of the performance exhibited by
TCMF
. This comparison underscores
TCMF
’s remarkable power to identify unique components from sparse noise accurately.

We also report the running time of each experiment in Table
3
. Compared with heuristic methods to robustly separate the shared and unique components,
TCMF
exhibits a slightly longer running time than RJIVE but significantly outperforms RaJIVE in terms of speed. The comparison highlights
TCMF
’s superior performance with moderate computation demands.
Although Robust PCA demonstrates a relatively short running time in this instance, larger-scale experiments presented in Appendix
B

will show that Robust PCA has larger running time scaling as the problem size increases.

7.3
Case Study: Defect Detection on Steel Surface

Hot rolling is an important process in steel manufacturing. For better product quality, a critical task is to detect and locate the defects that arise in the rolling process
(
Jin et al., 2000
)
. In this study, the dataset
(
Jin et al., 2000
;
Yan et al., 2018
)
comes from the HotEye video of a rolling steel plate. The video captures sharp pictures of the surface of the steel plate. An example is shown in the left graph of Figure
3
. The irregular dark dots in the graph indicate surface defects that require subsequent investigations
(
Jin et al., 2000
)
.

As different frames of the rolling video are related, they possess similar background patterns. Meanwhile, each frame also contains unique variations that reflect frame-by-frame differences. On top of the changing patterns, there are small defects on the surface of the steel plate. The defects, as shown in the left graph of Figure
3
, only occupy small spatial regions and thus can naturally be modeled by sparse outliers.

In such scenarios, the application of
TCMF
enables the identification of defects and extraction of common and unique patterns simultaneously. For this experiment, we use
TCMF
to segment 100 hot-rolling video frames. The right graph of Figure
3
illustrates two frames selected from the rolling video alongside the corresponding recovered global, local, and sparse components. We set the reduction parameter
\rho=0.97
and the number of epochs
T=100
. The details of the subroutine
JIMF
are relegated to Appendix
A.1
. Additionally, as a comparative analysis, we employ nonconvex robust PCA
(
Netrapalli et al., 2014
)
to recover and display the low-rank and sparse components from frames. Our robust PCA implementation alternatively applies SVD and hard thresholding. We require all entries in the sparse component to be negative in the hard-thresholding step to encode the domain knowledge that surface defects tend to have lower temperatures. The hyper-parameters for SVD and thresholding are consistent for both
TCMF
and Robust PCA.

Figure 3
:
Left
: An example of the surface of the steel bar. There are a few anomalies inside the red ellipse.
Right
: Recovered sparse noises, shared components, and unique components from
2
frames.

In Figure
3
, we can see that
TCMF
effectively identifies the small defects on the steel plate surface. The global component reflects the general patterns in the video frames, while the local component accentuates the variations in different frames. In contrast, the sparse components recovered by robust PCA do not faithfully represent the surface defects.

We proceed to show that
TCMF
-recovered sparse components can be conveniently leveraged for frame-level anomaly detection. Our task here is to identify which frames contain surface anomalies. Inspired by the statistics-based anomaly detection
(
Chandola et al., 2009
)
, we construct simple test statistics to monitor the anomalies. The test statistics is defined as the
\ell_{1}
norm of the recovered sparse noise on each frame
\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{1}
. Indeed, a large
\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{1}
provides strong evidence for surface defects. The choice of
\ell_{1}
norm is not special as we find other norms, such as
\ell_{2}
norm, would yield a similar performance.

After using
TCMF
to extract the sparse components, we calculate the test statistics for each frame. Then, we can set up a simple threshold-based classification rule for anomaly detection: when the
\ell_{1}
norm exceeds the threshold, we report an anomaly in the corresponding frame. In the case study, the threshold is set to be the highest value in the first
50
frames, which is the in-control group that does not contain anomalies
(
Yan et al., 2018
)
. We plot the test statistics and thresholds in Figure
5
. The blue dots and red crosses denote the (ground truth) normal and abnormal frame labels in

Yan et al. (2018)
. In an ideal plot of test statistics, one would expect the abnormal samples to have higher
\ell_{1}
norms, while normal samples should have lower norms. This is indeed the case for Figure
5
, where a simple threshold based on the sparse features can distinguish abnormal samples from normal ones with high accuracy.

In comparison, we also calculate the
\ell_{1}
norm of sparse noise recovered by robust PCA and plot the testing statistics in Figure
5
. In Figure
5
, the
\ell_{1}
norm is less indicative of anomaly labels, as some abnormal samples have small test statistics, while some normal samples have large statistics. It is also hard to use a threshold on the test statistics to predict anomalies.

Figure 4
:
Test statistics of robust PCA.

Figure 5
:
Test statistics of
TCMF
.

The comparison highlights
TCMF
’s ability to find surface defects. The results are understandable as
TCMF
uses a more refined model to decompose the thermal frames into three parts, thus having more representation power to fit the underlying physics in the manufacturing process. As a result, the recovered sparse components are more representative of the anomalies.

8
Conclusion

In this work, we propose a systematic method
TCMF
to separate shared, unique, and noise components from noisy observation matrices.
TCMF
is the first algorithm that is provably convergent to the ground truth under identifiability conditions that require the three components to have “small overlaps”.
TCMF
outperforms previous heuristic algorithms by large margins in numerical experiments and finds interesting applications in video segmentation, anomaly detection, and time series imputation.

Our work also opens up several venues for future theoretical exploration in separating shared and unique low-rank features from noisy matrices. For example, a minimax lower bound on the
\mu,\theta
, and
\alpha
can help fathom the statistical difficulty of such separation. Also, as many existing methods for
JIMF
rely on good initialization to excel, designing efficient algorithms for
JIMF
that are independent of the initialization is also an interesting topic. On the practical side, methods to integrate
TCMF
with other machine learning models, e.g., auto-encoders, to find nonlinear features in data are worth exploring.

This research is supported in part by Raed Al Kontar’s NSF CAREER Award 2144147 and Salar Fattahi’s NSF CAREER Award CCF-2337776, NSF Award DMS-2152776, and ONR Award N00014-22-1-2127,

Appendix A
Details of subroutine algorithm

In this section, we will elaborate on two subroutine algorithms in literature to solve the problem (
8
), specifically known as
HMF
and
PerPCA
. Among a plethora of existing methodologies aiming to distinguish shared and unique features, these two exhibit an exceptional significance, as they are proved to converge to the optimal resolution of problem (
8
) linearly under appropriate initial conditions. We underscore that the usage of
JIMF
is not restricted solely to these two methods. In essence, any algorithm with the ability to segregate common and unique components can be effectively employed as
JIMF
.

A.1
Heterogeneous matrix factorization

Heterogeneous matrix factorization (
HMF
)
(
Shi et al., 2023
)
is an algorithm proposed to solve the following problem,

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{U}_{g},\{\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}\}_{i=1,\cdots,N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\mathbf{U}_{g},\{\mathbf{V}_{(i),g},\mathbf{U}_{(i),l},\mathbf{V}_{(i),l}\}\right)

(16)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\mathbf{U}_{g}\mathbf{V}_{(i),g}^{T}-\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}\mathbf{V}_{(i),l}^{T}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{U}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{g}-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\text{subject to }

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}=\bm{0},\,\forall i.

Compared with (
8
), the objective in (
16
) contains two additional regularization terms
\frac{\beta}{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{U}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{g}-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}
. The regularization terms enhance the smoothness of the optimization objective thereby facilitating convergence. Despite the regularization terms, any optimal solution to (
16
) is also an optimal solution to (
8
). We can prove the claim in the following proposition.

Proposition 9
.

Let
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\}
be one set of optimal solutions to (
16
), then
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\}
is also a set of optimal solution to (
8
)

Proof.

The proof is straightforward. We first claim that
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}}=\mathbf{I}
and
\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}=\mathbf{I}
. We prove the claim by contradiction. Suppose otherwise, we can find a QR decomposition of
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}
as
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}}=\mathbf{Q}_{g}\mathbf{R}_{g}
and
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}}=\mathbf{Q}_{(i),l}\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}
, where
\mathbf{Q}_{g}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{(i),l}
’s are orthonormal and
\mathbf{R}_{g}
and
\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}
’s are upper-triangular. Furthermore, not both
\mathbf{R}_{g}
and
\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}
are identity matrices, thus
\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{g}-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}>0
. Now, we construct a refined set of solutions as,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF},refined}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Q}_{g}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF},refined}_{(i),g}

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{\texttt{HMF}}\mathbf{R}_{g}^{T}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF},refined}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Q}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF},refined}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{(i),l}_{\texttt{HMF}}\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}^{T}.

Then it’s easy to verify that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF},refined}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF},refined}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF},refined}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF},refined}_{(i),l}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\right)-\frac{\beta}{2}\left(\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{g}-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle<\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\right),

which contradicts with the global optimality of
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\}
. This proves the claim.

From the orthogonality, we know
f_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\right)=\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\right)
.

Now suppose
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\}
is not an optimal solution to (
8
). Then, we can find a different set of feasible solution
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{JIMF}},\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\}
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\right)

\displaystyle<\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\right).

We can similarly define a set of refined solutions

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Q}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}_{(i),g}

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{\texttt{JIMF}}\mathbf{R}_{g}^{\texttt{JIMF}}{}^{T}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Q}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{(i),l}_{\texttt{HMF}}\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}^{\texttt{JIMF}}{}^{T},

where
\mathbf{Q}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g}
,
\mathbf{R}_{g}^{\texttt{JIMF}}
,
\mathbf{Q}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}
,
\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}^{\texttt{JIMF}}
are QR decompositions that satisfy
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}=\mathbf{Q}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g}\mathbf{R}_{g}^{\texttt{JIMF}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}_{(i),l}=\mathbf{Q}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{R}_{(i),l}^{\texttt{JIMF}}
. Based on the refined set of solutions, we can prove that,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refined}_{(i),l}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\right)

\displaystyle<\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{f}_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\right),

which contradicts the optimality of
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{HMF}},\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{HMF}}_{(i),l}\}
.

This completes the proof.
∎

HMF
optimizes the objective by gradient descent. To ensure feasibility,
HMF
employs a special correction step to orthogonalize
\mathbf{U}_{g}
and
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}
without changing the objective at every step. The pseudo-code is presented in Algorithm
2
.

Algorithm 2

JIMF
by heterogeneous matrix factorization

1:

Input matrices
\{\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, stepsize
\eta_{\tau}
, iteration budget
R
.

2:

Initialize
\mathbf{U}_{g,1},\mathbf{V}_{(i),g,\frac{1}{2}},\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\frac{1}{2}},\mathbf{V}_{(i),l,1}
to be small random matrices.

3:

for

Iteration
\tau=1,...,R

do

4:

for
index
i=1,\cdots,N

do

5:

Correct
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau}=\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau-\frac{1}{2}}-\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}\left(\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau-\frac{1}{2}}

6:

Correct
\mathbf{V}_{(i),g,\tau}=\mathbf{V}_{(i),g,\tau-\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbf{V}_{(i),l,\tau}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau-\frac{1}{2}}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}\left(\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}\right)^{-1}

7:

Update
\mathbf{U}_{(i),g,\tau+1}=\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\nabla_{\mathbf{U}_{g}}\tilde{f}_{i}

8:

Update
\mathbf{V}_{(i),g,\tau+\frac{1}{2}}=\mathbf{V}_{(i),g,\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\nabla_{\mathbf{V}_{(i),g}}\tilde{f}_{i}

9:

Update
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau+\frac{1}{2}}=\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\nabla_{\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}}\tilde{f}_{i}

10:

Update
\mathbf{V}_{(i),l,\tau+1}=\mathbf{V}_{(i),l,\tau}-\eta_{\tau}\nabla_{\mathbf{V}_{(i),l}}\tilde{f}_{i}

11:

end

for

12:

Calculates
\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau+1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{U}_{(i),g,\tau+1}

13:

end

for

14:

Return
\mathbf{U}_{g,R},\{\mathbf{V}_{(i),g,R}\},\{\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,R}\},\{\mathbf{V}_{(i),l,R}\}
.

In Algorithm
2
, we use
\tau
to denote the iteration index, where the half-integer index denotes the update of the variable is half complete: it is updated by gradient descent but is not feasible yet. It is proven that under a group of sufficient conditions, Algorithm
2
converges to the optimal solutions of problem (
16
). The sufficient conditions require the stepsize
\eta_{\tau}
to be chosen appropriately and the initialization close to the optimal solution
(
Shi et al., 2023
)
.

In practice, Algorithm
2
is often efficient and accurate. Therefore, we implement
HMF
as the subroutine
JIMF
for all of our numerical simulations in Section
7
. To initialize Algorithm
2
, we adopt a spectral initialization approach. Specifically, we concatenate all matrices column-wise to form
\mathbf{M}^{concat}=[\mathbf{M}_{(1)},\mathbf{M}_{(2)},\cdots,\mathbf{M}_{(N)}]
. Subsequently, we perform a Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) on the concatenated matrix
\mathbf{M}^{concat}
to extract the top
r_{1}
column singular vectors, which serve as the initialization for
\mathbf{U}_{g,1}
in Algorithm
2
. Utilizing the calculated
\mathbf{U}_{g,1}
, we deflate
\mathbf{M}_{(i)}
by subtracting the projection of
\mathbf{M}_{(i)}
onto
\mathbf{U}_{g,1}
, denoted as
\mathbf{M}_{(i)}^{deflate}=\mathbf{M}_{(i)}-\mathbf{U}_{g,1}\mathbf{U}_{g,1}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{(i)}
. We then conduct another SVD to identify the top
r_{2}
singular vectors of
\mathbf{M}_{(i)}^{deflate}
, which are utilized as the initialization for
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\frac{1}{2}}
. The initializations for the coefficient matrices are established as
\mathbf{V}_{(i),g,\frac{1}{2}}=\mathbf{M}_{(i)}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{g,1}
and
\mathbf{V}_{(i),l,1}=\mathbf{M}_{(i)}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\frac{1}{2}}
.

The stepsize
\eta
in Algorithm
2
is individually adjusted for each dataset to achieve the fastest convergence. We choose a large total number of iterations
R
to ensure a small optimization error
\epsilon
.
Specifically, in the synthetic data, we set the stepsize to
0.005
and
R=500
. In the video segmentation task, we set the stepsize to
5\times 10^{-6}
and
R=200
. And on the hot rolling data, we set the stepsize to
4\times 10^{-5}
and
R=500
. In our experiments, we observe that the regularization parameter
\beta
exerts a negligible influence on the convergence of Algorithm
2
. Consequently, we maintain
\beta
within the range of
10^{-6}
to
10^{-5}
in all our experiments.

A.2
Personalized PCA

Personalized PCA
(
Shi and Kontar, 2024
)
is another subroutine to solve (
8
). More specifically, personalized PCA seeks to find orthonormal features
\mathbf{U}_{g}
and
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}
to minimize the residual of fitting, as shown in the following objective,

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{U}_{g},\{\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}\}_{i=1,\cdots,N}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\mathbf{U}_{g}\mathbf{U}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

(17)

\displaystyle\text{subject to }

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{g}=\mathbf{I},\,\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}=\mathbf{I},\,\mathbf{U}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}=\bm{0},\,\forall i.

The objective only optimizes the feature matrices
\mathbf{U}_{g}
and
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}
, but it’s essentially equivalent to problem (
8
). The formal statement is presented in the following proposition.

Proposition 10
.

Let
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{PerPCA}},\{\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{(i),l}\}
be one set of optimal solutions to (
17
), then
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{PerPCA}},\{\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{PerPCA}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}\}
is also a set of optimal solution to (
8
)

Proof.

We will also prove the proposition by contradiction. If
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{PerPCA}},\{\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{PerPCA}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}\}
is not a set of optimal solution to (
8
), we can find a different set of feasible solutions
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{JIMF}},\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\}
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\right)

\displaystyle<\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{PerPCA}},\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{\texttt{PerPCA}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{g}\mathbf{U}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{g}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{U}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{(i),l}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}.

If we fix
\mathbf{U}_{g}
and
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}
to be
\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g}
and
\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}
in problem (
8
), then the optimal solution of
\mathbf{V}_{(i),g}
and
\mathbf{V}_{(i),l}
is
\mathbf{V}^{\texttt{JIMF},opt}_{(i),g}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g}\right)^{-1}
and
\mathbf{V}^{\texttt{JIMF},opt}_{(i),l}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\right)^{-1}
. As a result,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\right)^{-1}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g}\right)^{-1}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF},opt}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF},opt}_{(i),l}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\right)

\displaystyle<\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{g}\mathbf{U}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{g}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{U}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}_{(i),l}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}.

If we define
\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refine}_{(i),l}=\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),l}\right)^{-1/2}
and
\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refine}_{(i),g}=\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g}{}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF}}_{(i),g}\right)^{-1/2}
, then
\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refine}_{(i),l}
and
\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\texttt{JIMF},refine}_{(i),g}
are also feasible for (
17
) and achieve lower objective. This contradicts the optimality of
\mathbf{U}_{g}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}
and
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}^{\texttt{PerPCA}}
.

∎

To solve the constrained optimization problem (
17
), personalized PCA adopts a distributed version of Stiefel gradient descent. The pseudo-code is presented in Algorithm
3
.

Algorithm 3

JIMF
by personalized PCA

Input observation matrices
\{\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, stepsize
\eta_{\tau}
, iteration budget
R
.

Initialize
\mathbf{U}_{g,1}
, and
\mathbf{U}_{(1),l,\frac{1}{2}},\cdots,\mathbf{U}_{(N),l,\frac{1}{2}}
.

Calculate
\mathbf{S}_{(i)}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}
for each
i
.

for
iteration
\tau=1,...,R

do

for
index
i=1,\cdots,N

do

Correct
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau}=\mathcal{GR}_{\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau-\frac{1}{2}}}\left(-\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

Calculate
\mathbf{G}_{(i),\tau}=\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}^{T}-\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau}^{T}\right)\left(\bm{S}_{(i)}\left[\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau},\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau}\right]\right)

Update
\mathbf{U}_{(i),g,\tau+1}=\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}+\eta_{\tau}(\mathbf{G}_{(i),\tau})_{1:d,1:r_{1}}

Update
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau+\frac{1}{2}}=\mathcal{GR}_{\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,\tau}}\left(\eta_{\tau}(\mathbf{G}_{(i),\tau})_{1:d,(r_{1}+1):(r_{1}+r_{2,(i)})}\right)

end

for

Update
\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau+1}=\mathcal{GR}_{\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{U}_{(i),g,\tau+1}-\mathbf{U}_{g,\tau}\right)

end

for

Calculate
\mathbf{V}_{(i),g,R}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{g,R}
and
\mathbf{V}_{(i),l,R}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,R}
.

Return
\mathbf{U}_{g,R},\{\mathbf{V}_{(i),g,R}\},\{\mathbf{U}_{(i),l,R}\},\{\mathbf{V}_{(i),l,R}\}
.

In Algorithm
3
,
\mathcal{GR}
denotes generalized retraction. In practice, it can be implemented via polar projection
\mathcal{GR}_{\mathbf{U}}\left(\mathbf{V}\right)=\left(\mathbf{U}+\mathbf{V}\right)\left(\mathbf{U}^{T}\mathbf{U}+\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{U}+\mathbf{U}^{T}\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{V}^{T}\mathbf{V}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Algorithm
3
can also be proved to converge to the optimal solutions with suitable choices of stepsize and initialization
(
Shi and Kontar, 2024
)
.

Appendix B
Additional running time comparisons

In this section, we include the additional running time comparison between
TCMF
and Robust PCA. We use a set of synthetic datasets with varying numbers of sources
N
, then compare the per-iteration running time of the two algorithms. More specifically, we follow the setting in Section
7.1
where
n_{1}=15
and
n_{2}=1000
, and generate synthetic datasets where the number of sources
N
changes from
100
to
10000
. Then, we apply
TCMF
and Robust PCA on the same dataset. We do not parallelize computations for either algorithm for fair comparison. The per-iteration running time of the two algorithms is collected and plotted in Figure
6
.

Figure 6
:
Running time comparison between runtime of
TCMF
and Robuct PCA.

From Figure
6
, it is clear that the running time of
TCMF
scales linearly with the number of sources
N
, which is consistent with the complexity analysis.

In contrast, though Robuct PCA has a smaller per-iteration runtime when
N
is small, as
N
becomes larger, the runtime increases faster than
TCMF
. This is because Robuct PCA vectorizes the observation matrices from each source. The resulting vector from each source has dimension
n_{1}n_{2}
. Robuct PCA then concatenates these vectors into a
n_{1}n_{2}\times N
matrix and alternatively performs Singular Value Decomposition and hard-thresholding. For each application of SVD, the computational complexity is
\mathcal{O}(n_{1}n_{2}(N^{2}+N)+N^{3})
when
N\leq n_{1}n_{2}

(
Li et al., 2019
)
. Indeed, in Figure
6
, the slope of the initial part of the Robust PCA curve is around
1.2
, and the slope of the final part is around
3.0
, suggesting that the running time scales cubically in the large
N
regime.

Such comparison highlights
TCMF
’s computational advantage when
N
is large.

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
5

In this section, we will introduce the details of the proof of Theorem
5
. We will firstly introduce a few basic lemmas, then prove the KKT conditions in Lemma
7
. Based on the KKT conditions, we introduce an infinite series to represent the solutions to (
8
). Next, we will prove Lemma
20
, which is a formal version of Lemma
6
. Finally, we will use induction to prove Theorem
21
, which is the formal version of Theorem
5
.

Remember that we use
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}
to denote
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}={\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}
, where
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}
and
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}
are the global and local components for source
i
defined as

{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}={\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{V}^{\star}}_{(i),g}^{T}
and
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}={\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}{\mathbf{V}^{\star}}_{(i),l}^{T}
. We assume all nonzero singular values of
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}
are lower bounded by
\sigma_{\min}>0
and upper bounded by
\sigma_{\max}>0
. As introduced in the proof sketch, we use
\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}={\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}-\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),t}
to denote the difference between our estimate of the sparse noise at epoch
t
and the ground truth. The following notations will be used throughout our proof:

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{(i)}=\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}^{T}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}^{T}+\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}^{T},\ \ i\in[N],\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{F}_{(0)}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{F}_{(i)}

(18)

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{(i)}={\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}^{T},\ \ i\in[N],\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{T}_{(0)}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}.

(19)

Since in the ground truth model, the SVD of
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}
can be written as
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}=\left[{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g},{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right]\text{diag}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{(i),g},\mathbf{\Sigma}_{(i),l})\left[{\mathbf{W}^{\star}}_{(i),g},{\mathbf{W}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right]^{T}
, one can immediately see that
\mathbf{T}_{(i)}
’s nonzero eigenvalues are upper bounded by
\sigma_{\max}^{2}
and lower bounded by
\sigma_{\min}^{2}
. Finally, recall that we use
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}
,
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}
,
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}
, and
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l}
to denote the optimal solutions to (
8
) (we omit the subscript
t
here for brevity.) For a series of square matrices of the same shape
\mathbf{A}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{A}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, we use
\prod_{m=1}^{k}\mathbf{A}_{m}
to denote the product of these matrices in the ascending order of indices, and
\prod_{m=k}^{1}\mathbf{A}_{m}
to denote the product of these matrices in the descending order of indices,

\displaystyle\prod_{m=1}^{k}\mathbf{A}_{m}=\mathbf{A}_{1}\mathbf{A}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{A}_{k-1}\mathbf{A}_{k}

\displaystyle\prod_{m=k}^{1}\mathbf{A}_{m}=\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{A}_{k-1}\cdots\mathbf{A}_{2}\mathbf{A}_{1}.

Our next two lemmas provide upper bound on the maximum row-norm of the errors with respect to the
\ell_{\infty}
-norms of
\mathbf{E}_{(i)}
. By building upon these two lemmas, we provide a key result in Lemma
13
connecting
\{\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\}
and the error matrices
\{\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\}
.

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{E}_{(1)},\cdots,\mathbf{E}_{(N)},\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}
are
\alpha
-sparse and
\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times r}
is
\mu
-incoherent and
\left\lVert U\right\rVert\leq 1
. For any integers
p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{k}\geq 0
, and
i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{k}\in\{0,1,\cdots,N\}
, we have

\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)\mathbf{U}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{2(p_{1}+p_{2}+\cdots+p_{k})}.

(20)

With a slight abuse of notation, in Lemma
11
and the rest of the paper, we define
\mathbf{E}_{(0)}\mathbf{E}_{(0)}^{T}
to be,

\mathbf{E}_{(0)}\mathbf{E}_{(0)}^{T}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N}\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\mathbf{E}_{(i)}^{T}.

(21)

Proof.

We will prove it by induction on the exponent.
From the definition of incoherence, we know that when
p_{1}+\cdots+p_{k}=0
, the inequality (
20
) holds.
Now suppose that the inequality (
20
) holds for all
p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{k}\geq 0
such that
p_{1}+\cdots+p_{k}\leq s-1
and
i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{k}\in\{0,1,\cdots,N\}
. We will prove the statement for
p_{1}+\cdots+p_{k}=s
. Without loss of generality, we assume
p_{1}\geq 1
. One can write

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)\mathbf{U}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}=\sum_{l}\left(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)\mathbf{U}\mathbf{e}_{l}\right)^{2}

(22)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\left(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T})^{p_{1}-1}\left(\prod_{\ell=2}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)\mathbf{U}\mathbf{e}_{l}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\left(\sum_{h}\left[\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right]_{j,h}\mathbf{e}_{h}^{T}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T})^{p_{1}-1}\left(\prod_{\ell=2}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)\mathbf{U}\mathbf{e}_{l}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}}\left[\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right]_{j,h_{1}}\left[\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right]_{j,h_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\mathbf{e}_{h_{1}}^{T}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T})^{p_{1}-1}\left(\prod_{\ell=2}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)\mathbf{U}\mathbf{e}_{l}\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\mathbf{U}^{T}\left(\prod_{\ell=k}^{2}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T})^{p_{1}-1}\mathbf{e}_{h_{2}}

.

Since
\sum_{l}\mathbf{e}_{l}\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}=\mathbf{I}
, we can simplify the summation as,

\displaystyle\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\mathbf{e}_{h_{1}}^{T}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T})^{p_{1}-1}\left(\prod_{\ell=2}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{T}\left(\prod_{\ell=k}^{2}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T})^{p_{1}-1}\mathbf{e}_{h_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\max_{m}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{m}^{T}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T})^{p_{1}-1}\left(\prod_{\ell=2}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)\mathbf{U}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{2}}\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{4s-4},

where in the last step, we used the induction hypothesis. Now, to complete the proof, we consider two cases.

If
i_{1}>0
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{2}}=\sum_{h_{1},h_{2},g_{1},g_{1}}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})})_{j,g_{1}}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})})_{h_{1},g_{1}}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})})_{j,g_{2}}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})})_{h_{2},g_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\right\rVert_{\infty}\alpha n_{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\right\rVert_{\infty}\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\right\rVert_{\infty}\alpha n_{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{4},

where the last inequality holds because at most
\alpha n_{1}
entries in each column of
\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}
are nonzero and at most
\alpha n_{2}
entries in each row of
\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}
are nonzero.
On the other hand, if
i_{1}=0
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(0)}\mathbf{E}_{(0)}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(0)}\mathbf{E}_{(0)}^{T}\right)_{j,h_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}}\left(\sum_{f_{1}>0}\mathbf{E}_{(f_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(f_{1})}^{T}\right)_{jk_{1}}\left(\sum_{f_{2}>0}\mathbf{E}_{(f_{2})}\mathbf{E}_{(f_{2})}^{T}\right)_{jk_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}N^{2}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{4}.

Therefore, in both cases, we have,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\mathbf{U}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{4s},

for every possible
i_{1}
and every
j
. This concludes our proof.
∎

Next, we present a slightly different lemma.

Lemma 12
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{E}_{(1)},\cdots,\mathbf{E}_{(N)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}
are
\alpha
-sparse and
\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}\times r}
is
\mu
-incoherent. For any integers
p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{k}\geq 0
, and
i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{k}\in\{0,1,\cdots,N\}
, we have,

\displaystyle\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{\ell})}^{T})^{p_{\ell}}\right)\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k+1})}\mathbf{V}\right\rVert_{2}

(23)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{2(p_{1}+p_{2}+\cdots+p_{k})+1}.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma
11
, and hence, omitted for brevity.
∎

Combining Lemma
11
and
12
, we can show the following key lemma on the connection between
\{\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\}
and the error matrices
\{\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\}
.

Lemma 13
.

For every
i\in[N]
, suppose that

\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}
is
\alpha
-sparse and
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}={\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{W}^{\star}}_{(i)}
is rank-
r
with
\mu
-incoherent matrices
{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times r}
and
{\mathbf{W}^{\star}}_{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}\times r}
. For any integers
p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{k}\geq 0
, and
i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{k}\in\{0,1,\cdots,N\}
, the following holds for any
\mu
-incoherent matrix
\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times r}
,

\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{\ell})}^{p_{\ell}}\mathbf{U}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}+2\sigma_{\max})\right)^{p_{1}+p_{2}+\cdots+p_{k}},

(24)

where
\mathbf{F}_{(i)}
is defined as (
18
).

Proof.

We firstly expand
\mathbf{F}_{(i_{1})}^{p_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{2})}^{p_{2}}\cdots\mathbf{F}_{(i_{k})}^{p_{k}}\mathbf{U}
and rearrange the terms by the number of consecutive
\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\mathbf{E}_{(i)}^{T}
terms appearing in the beginning of each factor.

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{(i_{1})}^{p_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{2})}^{p_{2}}\cdots\mathbf{F}_{(i_{k})}^{p_{k}}\mathbf{U}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}({\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{i_{1}})^{T}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}+\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}({\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{i_{1}})^{T}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}+\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{2})}({\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{i_{2}})^{T}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{2})}^{T}+\mathbf{E}_{(i_{2})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{2})}^{T}\right)\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{2})}({\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{i_{2}})^{T}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{2})}^{T}+\mathbf{E}_{(i_{2})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{2})}^{T}\right)

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}({\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i_{k})})^{T}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i_{k})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}^{T}+\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}^{T}\right)\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}({\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i_{k})})^{T}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i_{k})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}^{T}+\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}^{T}\right)\mathbf{U}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{1}}\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{k})}^{T}\right)^{p_{k}}\mathbf{U}

\displaystyle+\sum_{r=0}^{p_{1}+\cdots+p_{k}-1}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{1}}\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{t-1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\right)^{r-(\sum_{g=1}^{t-1}p_{g})}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}^{T}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\right)\mathbf{F}_{(i_{t})}^{(\sum_{g=1}^{t}p_{g})-1-r}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{t+1})}^{p_{t+1}}\cdots\mathbf{F}_{(i_{k})}^{p_{k}}\mathbf{U}.

For the first term, by Lemma
11
, we have

\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{1}}\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{i_{k}}\mathbf{E}_{i_{k}}^{T}\right)^{p_{k}}\mathbf{U}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{2(p_{1}+\cdots+p_{k})}.

For the remaining terms, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{1}}\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{t-1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\right)^{r-(\sum_{g=1}^{t-1}p_{g})}

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}({\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})})^{T}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\right)\mathbf{F}_{(i_{t})}^{(\sum_{g=1}^{t}p_{g})-1-r}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{t+1})}^{p_{t+1}}\cdots\mathbf{F}_{(i_{k})}^{p_{k}}\mathbf{U}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{1}}\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{t-1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\right)^{r-(\sum_{g=1}^{t-1}p_{g})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}{\mathbf{W}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}

\displaystyle\times{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{t})}^{(\sum_{g=1}^{t}p_{g})-1-r}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{t+1})}^{p_{t+1}}\cdots\mathbf{F}_{(i_{k})}^{p_{k}}\mathbf{U}

\displaystyle+\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{1}}\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{t-1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\right)^{r-(\sum_{g=1}^{t-1}p_{g})}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}

\displaystyle\times{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}{\mathbf{W}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{t})}^{(\sum_{g=1}^{t}p_{g})-1-r}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{t+1})}^{p_{t+1}}\cdots\mathbf{F}_{(i_{k})}^{p_{k}}\mathbf{U}.

We can bound the two terms separately. By Lemma
12
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{1}}\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{t-1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\right)^{r-(\sum_{g=1}^{t-1}p_{g})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}{\mathbf{W}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{2r+1}.

And by Lemma
11
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{1}}\cdots\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t-1})}^{T}\right)^{p_{t-1}}\left(\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}\mathbf{E}_{(i_{t})}^{T}\right)^{r-(\sum_{g=1}^{t-1}p_{g})}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i_{t})}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{2r}.

For an
\alpha
-sparse matrix
\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}
, its operator norm is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{2}=\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1,\left\lVert\mathbf{h}\right\rVert=1}\mathbf{v}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\mathbf{h}=\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1,\left\lVert\mathbf{h}\right\rVert=1}\sum_{j,k}\mathbf{v}_{j}\mathbf{h}_{k}[\mathbf{E}_{(i)}]_{jk}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1,\left\lVert\mathbf{h}\right\rVert=1}\sum_{j,k}\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}}{n_{2}}}+\mathbf{h}_{k}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{n_{2}}{n_{1}}}\right)[\mathbf{E}_{(i)}]_{jk}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1,\left\lVert\mathbf{h}\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{j}\mathbf{v}_{j}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}}{n_{2}}}\alpha n_{2}+\sum_{k}\mathbf{h}_{k}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{n_{2}}{n_{1}}}\alpha n_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\alpha\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}.

Therefore
\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}
. As a result, we know,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 2\sigma_{\max}\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left(2\sigma_{\max}+\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right).

We thus have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{1})}^{p_{1}}\mathbf{F}_{(i_{2})}^{p_{2}}\cdots\mathbf{F}_{(i_{k})}^{p_{k}}{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\Big(\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{2(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{\ell})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{r=0}^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{\ell}-1}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{2r+1}

\displaystyle\times 2\sigma_{\max}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left(2\sigma_{\max}+\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)\right)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{\ell}-1-r}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\Big(\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{2\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{\ell}}

\displaystyle+\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{\ell}}\Big(\left(2\sigma_{\max}+\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{\ell}}

\displaystyle-\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{\ell}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{\ell}}\left(\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}+2\sigma_{\max}\right)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}p_{\ell}}.

This finishes our proof.
∎

Lemma
13
is an important lemma as it provides an upper bound on the maximum row norm of the product of a group of sparse matrices and an incoherent matrix. We will use Lemma
13
extensively when we calculate the
\ell_{\infty}
norm of error terms in the output of
JIMF
.

Next, we prove that the optimal solution indeed satisfies the KKT conditions delineated in Lemma
7
.

Proof of Lemma
7
The proof is presented in three parts. In the first part, we show that the optimal solution optimal
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}
,
\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l}\}
satisfies the linear independence constraint qualification (LICQ). This ensures that the optimal solution satisfies the KKT conditions. In the second part, we prove the validity of the equations in (
12
). Finally, we prove the correctness of the equations in (
13
).

Proof of LICQ.

We begin by showing
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}
has full column rank. By contradiction, suppose
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}
has rank
r_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}<r_{1}
. Since
\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}
has rank at least
r_{1}+r_{2}
, the residual
\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}-\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{T}-\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l}^{T}
has rank at least
1
. Therefore we can always find another
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{{}^{\prime}}
such that
f_{i}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{{}^{\prime}},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l})<f_{i}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l})
. This contradicts the fact that
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}
is optimal.

Next we will establish the LICQ of the constraints. We define
h_{ijk}
as the inner product between the
j
-th column of
\mathbf{U}_{g}
and the
k
-th column of
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}
,
h_{ijk}(\mathbf{x})=[\mathbf{U}_{g}]_{:,j}^{T}[\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}]_{:,k}
. The constraints in (
8
) can be rewritten as
h_{ijk}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})=0,\forall i\in[r_{1}],\forall j\in[r_{2}],\forall k\in[N]
. LICQ requires
\nabla h_{ijk}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})
to be linearly independent for all
ijk

(
Bertsekas, 1997
, Proposition 3.1.1)
.

Suppose we can find constants
\psi_{ijk}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{k=1}^{r_{2}}\psi_{ijk}\nabla h_{ijk}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})=0
. We consider the partial derivative of
h_{ijk}
over the
k^{{}^{\prime}}
-th column of
\mathbf{U}_{(i^{{}^{\prime}}),l}
. It is easy to derive,

\frac{\partial}{\partial[\mathbf{U}_{(i^{{}^{\prime}}),l}]_{:,k^{{}^{\prime}}}}h_{ijk}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})=\delta_{ii^{{}^{\prime}}}\delta_{kk^{{}^{\prime}}}[\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}]_{:,j},

where
\delta_{ii^{{}^{\prime}}}
is the Kronecker delta function. Then the constants
\psi_{ijk}
should satisfy,

\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\psi_{i^{{}^{\prime}}jk^{{}^{\prime}}}[\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}]_{:,j}=0.

As the columns of
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}
are linearly independent,
\psi_{i^{{}^{\prime}}jk^{{}^{\prime}}}=0
for each
j
. This holds for any
i^{{}^{\prime}}
and
k^{{}^{\prime}}
. Therefore
\psi_{ijk}=0
for all
i,j,k
. This implies
\nabla h_{ijk}
’s are linearly independent.

Proof of Equations (
12
).
The Lagrangian of the optimization problem (
8
) can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\mathbf{U}_{g}\mathbf{V}_{(i),g}^{T}+\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}\mathbf{V}_{(i),l}^{T}-\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

(25)

\displaystyle+\text{Tr}\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{8,(i)}\mathbf{U}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}\right),

where
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{8,(i)}
is the dual variable for the constraint
\mathbf{U}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}=0
.

Under the LICQ, we know that
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g},\{\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l},\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l}\}
satisfies KKT condition. Setting the gradient of
\mathcal{L}
with respect to
\mathbf{V}_{(i),g}
and
\mathbf{V}_{(i),l}
to zero, we can prove (
12d
) and (
12c
). Considering the constraint
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}=0
, we can solve them as
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\right)^{-1}
and
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\right)^{-1}
.
Then we examine the gradient of
\mathcal{L}
with respect to
\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}}\mathcal{L}=\left(\mathbf{U}_{g}\mathbf{V}_{(i),g}^{T}+\mathbf{U}_{(i),l}\mathbf{V}_{(i),l}^{T}-\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\right)\mathbf{V}_{(i),l}+\mathbf{U}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{(8),i}^{T}.

Substituting
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\right)^{-1}
and
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l}=\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\right)^{-1}
in the above gradient and setting it to zero, we have

\displaystyle\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\right)^{-1}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\right)^{-1}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}-\mathbf{I}\right)\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l}

\displaystyle+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{(8),i}^{T}=0.

Left multiplying both sides by
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}
, we have
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{8,(i)}=0
. Left multiplying both sides by
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}
, we have
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}-\mathbf{I}=0
. Therefore we also have
\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\right)^{-1}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\right)^{-1}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}-\mathbf{I}\right)\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\mathbf{V}_{(i),l}=0
.
This proves equation (
12b
).
Now, setting the derivative of
\mathcal{L}
with respect to
\mathbf{U}_{g}
to zero, we have

\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{U}_{g}}\mathcal{L}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}^{T}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),l}^{T}-\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\right)\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g}=0.

Left multiplying both sides by
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}
, we have
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}-\mathbf{I}=0
. We have thus proven (
12a
). This completes the proof for (
12
).

Proof of Equations (
13
).
Equation (
12b
) can be rewritten as:

\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}=\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}+\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}.

(26)

Since
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}
is positive definite, we can use
\mathbf{W}_{(i),l}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}\mathbf{W}_{(i),l}^{T}=\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}
to denote its eigen-decomposition, where
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{1}}
is a positive definite diagonal matrix and
\mathbf{W}_{(i),l}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{1}}
is orthonormal. Upon defining
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}=\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{W}_{(i),l}
,
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}
is also orthonormal as
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}=\mathbf{W}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{W}_{(i),l}=\mathbf{I}
.
Similarly, we rewrite the equation (
12a
) as:

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{M}_{(i)}\mathbf{M}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}=\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{M}_{(i)}\mathbf{M}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{(i)}\mathbf{M}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}.

(27)

Since
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}
is positive definite, we can use
\mathbf{W}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{g}^{T}=\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}
to denote its eigen decomposition, where
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{1}}
is positive diagonal,
\mathbf{W}_{g}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{1}}
is orthogonal
\mathbf{W}_{g}\mathbf{W}_{g}^{T}=\mathbf{W}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{W}_{g}=\mathbf{I}
.
We define
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}
as
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}=\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}\mathbf{W}_{g}
, then
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}
is also orthonormal. Additionally,
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}=\mathbf{W}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{W}_{(i),l}=0
. This completes the proof of equation (
13c
).

Next, we proceed with the proof of equations (
13b
) and (
13a
). By right multiplying both sides of (
27
) with
\mathbf{W}_{g}
and replacing
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g}
and
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{(i),l}
by
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}
and
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}
, we have

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}={\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}+\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}.

(28)

Similarly, by right multiplying both sides of (
26
) with
\mathbf{W}_{(i),l}
, we can rewrite (
26
) as,

\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}={\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}+{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}.

(29)

We thus prove the equations (
13b
) and (
13a
), where
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}={\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}
.
\hfill\blacksquare

We note that the KKT conditions provide a set of conditions that must be satisfied for all stationary points of (
8
). Our next key contribution is to use these conditions to characterize a few interesting properties satisfied by all the optimal solutions. To this goal, we heavily rely on the spectral properties of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}
,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}
, and
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}
.

For simplicity, we introduce three additional notations,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{4,(i)}=-\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}
,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{5,(i)}=-\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}^{T}/N
, and
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{1}}
defined as,

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}=\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}^{-1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}^{T}.

(30)

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}
is a symmetric matrix. It is worth noting that
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}
is well defined as the diagonal matrix
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}
is invertible throughput the proof. We also introduce short-hand notation
\Delta\mathbf{P}_{g}
to denote
\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}
and
\Delta\mathbf{P}_{(i),l}
to denote
\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}
.

Spectral properties of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}
,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}
,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}
, and
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}
are critical for developing the solutions to KKT conditions. We will establish these properties in the following lemmas.

Before diving into these properties, we investigate the deviance of the estimates features
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}
and
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}
to ground truth features
{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}
and
{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}
.

Lemma 14
.

If
\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 4\sigma_{\max}\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}
, and
\mathbf{E}_{(i)}
is
\alpha
-sparse with

\alpha\leq\frac{1}{60\mu^{4}r^{2}}\frac{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}{\sigma_{\max}^{4}}\left(1+\frac{4\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sqrt{\theta}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)^{-2}
, we have,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\frac{5\sigma_{\max}}{\sqrt{\theta}\sigma_{\min}^{2}},

(31)

and

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert\leq 3\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+4\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sqrt{\theta}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right).

(32)

Proof.

By
Shi and Kontar (2024, Theorem 1)
, we know that
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}
and
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}
corresponding to the global optimal solutions to the problem (
8
) satisfy

\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{4}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}{\theta\sigma_{\min}^{4}}.

(33)

Note that the norm of the error term
\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}
is bounded by:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}=\left\lVert{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}^{T}+\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}{{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}}^{T}+\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}^{T}\right\rVert_{F}

(34)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{F}\left(2\left\lVert{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left(2\sigma_{\max}+\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right).

Therefore, we know from (
33
) that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}}{\sqrt{\theta\sigma_{\min}^{4}}}\leq\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left(2\sigma_{\max}+\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)\frac{2}{\sqrt{\theta\sigma_{\min}^{4}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\frac{5\sigma_{\max}}{\sqrt{\theta}\sigma_{\min}^{2}},

where we used the condition
\alpha{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\sigma_{\max}/2
for the last inequality.

From (
8
), we can also deduce that that the column vectors of
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}
span the top invariant subspace of
\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)
. Since column vectors of
{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}
span the top invariant subspace of
\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}\right){\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}\right)
, we know from Weyl’s theorem
(
Tao, 2010
)
and Davis-Khan theorem
(
Rinaldo, 2017
)
that,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left\lVert\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)-\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}\right){\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}\right)\right\rVert_{F}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}-\left\lVert\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)-\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}\right){\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}\right)\right\rVert}.

Since

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)-\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}\right){\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}\right)\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)\left(\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}^{T}-{\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}^{T}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert{\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}^{T}\left(\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right)\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\left(\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right){\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}{\mathbf{M}^{\star}}_{(i)}^{T}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{F}+2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\sigma_{\max}\left(1+4\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sqrt{\theta}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}{6},

we have,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}{6}}\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\sigma_{\max}\left(1+4\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sqrt{\theta}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+4\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sqrt{\theta}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right).

This completes the proof.

∎

With Lemma
14
, we first provide upper bound on the operator norm of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}
.

Lemma 15
.

For every
i\in[N]
, suppose that
\mathbf{U}^{\star}_{(i),l}
’s are
\theta
-misaligned,
\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 4\sigma_{\max}\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}
, and
\mathbf{E}_{(i)}
is
\alpha
-sparse with
\alpha\leq\frac{1}{10\mu^{2}r}
, we have,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}\right\rVert\leq 2\sigma_{\max}.

Proof.

From the KKT condition (
13
), we know,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}\right\rVert=\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right){\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert\leq 2\sigma_{\max}.

This completes the proof.
∎

We then estimate lower bounds on the smallest eigenvalues of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}
,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}
, and
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}
. These estimates rely on more refined matrix perturbation analysis.

Lemma 16
.

For every
i\in[N]
, suppose that
\mathbf{U}^{\star}_{(i),l}
’s are
\theta
-misaligned,
\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 4\sigma_{\max}\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}
, and
\mathbf{E}_{(i)}
is
\alpha
-sparse with

\alpha\leq\frac{1}{64}\frac{1}{\mu^{4}r^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\sigma_{\max}}\right)^{4}\left(1+2\left(\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}}\right)^{2}+\frac{8}{\sqrt{\theta}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}}\right)^{4}\right)^{-2}.

The minimum eigenvalues of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}
and
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}
are lower bounded by
\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}
.

Proof.

This lemma is a result of Weyl’s theorem
(
Tao, 2010
)
and the perturbation bound on the eigenspaces.
From the first equation in (
13
), we know,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}={\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}\bm{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}.

Therefore,
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}
’s columns are the eigenvectors of the symmetric matrix
\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}
, with eigenvalues corresponding to the diagonal entries of
\bm{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}
.
According to the definition of
\mathbf{T}_{(i)}
, we know that the eigenvalues of
\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}={\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i),l}{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\star}}_{(i),l}^{2}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i),l}^{T}
are lower bounded by
\sigma_{\min}^{2}
. Hence, as a result of Weyl’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}\right)=\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{\min}^{2}-\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert_{2}.

On the other hand, by triangle inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\ \ +\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{\max}^{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left(2\sigma_{\max}+\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{5}{2}\sqrt{\alpha}{\overline{n}}\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+4\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\sqrt{\theta}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2},

where we used the fact
\left\lVert\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\right\rVert\leq\sigma_{\max}^{2}
in the third inequality, Lemma
14
in the 4th inequality, and the assumed upper bound on
\alpha
in the last inequality. We thus have
\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}\right)\geq\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}
.

Similarly, we can solve
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}
from the first equation of (
13
) as
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{3,(i)}={\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right){\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}
. Plugging this into the second equation of (
13
), we have

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right){\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}={\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}.

Thus, the columns of
{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}
are the eigenvectors of the matrix
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)
, with eigenvalues corresponding to the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}
. Again, since the minimum eigenvalue of
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right)\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right)
is lower bounded by
\sigma_{\min}^{2}
, Weyl’s inequality can be invoked to provide a lower bound on the minimum eigenvalue of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}
:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\right)=\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{\min}^{2}-

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right)\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right)-\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)}{N}\right\rVert_{2}.

The operator norm on the right hand side can be bounded by triangle inequalities. For each term in the summation, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right)\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right)-\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}+\mathbf{F}_{(i)}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right)\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right)-\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}\right)\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}}\right)\right\rVert
