\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right\rVert\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}\left\lVert\mathbf{T}_{(0)}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}^{-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}^{-1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{5,(i)}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}^{-1}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{\max}\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right\rVert\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right).

Bounding the sixth term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g,0}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\sum_{l}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\left(\mathbf{T}_{(0)}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}^{-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{T}_{(i)}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}^{-1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{5,(i)}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{6}^{-1}\right)^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right\rVert\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty},

where we applied the incoherence condition on
\mathbf{T}_{(0)}
and
\mathbf{T}_{(i)}
, and the relation
\sum_{l}\left|\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|\leq\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty}
.

Bounding the seventh term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}^{T}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}^{T}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right\rVert\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}\sigma_{\max},

where we applied the incoherence on
{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}
and
\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right\rVert\leq 1
in the first inequality.

Bounding the eighth term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\sum_{l}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{l}\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j,l}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right\rVert\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left(\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right\rVert\right)^{2}\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty},

where we applied
\sum_{l}\left|\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|\leq\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty}
in the first inequality.

Bounding the ninth term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}^{T}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left(\mathbf{T}_{(i)}\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}^{-1}+{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g,0}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{4,(i)}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2,(i)}^{-1}\right)\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}^{T}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{2}r}{\overline{n}}\sigma_{\max}\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}+\left(\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert,

where we applied the incoherence condition of
\mathbf{T}_{(0)}
and
\mathbf{T}_{(i)}
.

Bounding the tenth term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\sum_{l}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{l}\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle=\max_{j,l}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{l}\right|\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}+\left(\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty},

where we applied the incoherence condition in the first inequality, (
57
) in the second inequality.

Bounding the eleventh term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}^{T}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}^{T}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\frac{\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}+\left(\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}},

where we applied the incoherence condition in the first inequality, and (
57
) in the second inequality.

Bounding the twelfth term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\sum_{l}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{l}\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),l}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j,l}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{l}\right|\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right|\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{1}}}\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}+\left(\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty},

where we applied the condition
\sum_{l}\left|\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|\leq\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty}
in the first inequality, the incoherence condition in the second inequality.

Bounding the thirteenth term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}^{T}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right\rVert_{\infty}

(70)

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}^{T}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}},

where we apply the incoherence condition in the first inequality, and
\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\leq 1
in the second inequality.

Bounding the fourteenth term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\sum_{m}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{m}\mathbf{e}_{m}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j.m}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{m}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\right)^{2}\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty},

where we applied the condition
\sum_{l}\left|\mathbf{e}_{l}^{T}\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|\leq\alpha n_{1}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty}
in the first inequality.

Bounding the fifteenth term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{j,k}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left({\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g,0}+\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right){\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{(i),l}{\mathbf{W}^{\star}}_{(i),l}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{j}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g,0}\right|+\max_{j}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right|\right)\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{j}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g,0}\right|+\max_{j}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right|\right)\left\lVert\Delta\mathbf{P}_{g}\right\rVert\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{2}r}{\overline{n}}\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)\left\lVert\Delta\mathbf{P}_{g}\right\rVert\sigma_{\max}+\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}\left\lVert\Delta\mathbf{P}_{g}\right\rVert\max_{j}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\right|.

The second inequality comes from the relation
\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right\rVert=\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}^{T}\right){\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right\rVert=\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}\left(\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}^{T}\right){\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}^{T}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}(\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}-{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}^{T})\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}-{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}^{T}\right\rVert
.

Bounding the sixteenth term of (
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\max_{j,k}\left|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\left({\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),0}+\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right){\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}^{T}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{\star}}_{(i),g}{\mathbf{W}^{\star}}_{(i),g}^{T}\mathbf{e}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}\right\rVert+\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\right)\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}^{T}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}\right\rVert\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l,0}\right\rVert+\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\right)\left\lVert\Delta\mathbf{P}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu^{2}r}{\overline{n}}\sigma_{\max}\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}+\left(\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\left\lVert\Delta\mathbf{P}_{(i),l}\right\rVert

(71)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\Delta\mathbf{P}_{(i),l}\right\rVert\sigma_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{n_{2}}}\max_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{e}_{j}^{T}\delta\mathbf{H}_{(i),l}\right\rVert

(TM16)

,
where we again applied Lemma
19
in the first inequality. The second inequality comes from the relation
\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}^{T}{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}\right\rVert=\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}^{T}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\right){\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}\right\rVert=\left\lVert{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{(i),l}^{T}\left(\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}}\right){\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}}-\mathbf{P}_{{\mathbf{H}^{\star}}_{g}}\right\rVert
.

Combining these sixteen terms (
)-(
TM16
) and considering the fact that
\alpha\leq 1
, we have,

\left\lVert{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}-\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{\alpha}\mu^{2}r\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\right\rVert_{\infty}C_{4},

where

\displaystyle C_{4}=34327\left(\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)^{9}+534\left(\frac{2\sigma_{\max}^{2}}{\frac{3}{4}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)^{5}\theta^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\kappa^{9}+\kappa^{5}\theta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

(72)

This completes our proof.
∎

Finally we will prove Theorem
5
. We will first state its formal version below.

Theorem 21
.

Suppose that the conditions of Lemma
19
are satisfied. Additionally, suppose that there exists a constant
0<\rho_{\min}<1
such that
\alpha\leq\frac{\rho_{\min}^{2}}{4\mu^{4}r^{2}C_{4}^{2}}
. Then, the following statements hold at iteration
t\geq 1
of Algorithm
1
with
\lambda_{1}=\frac{\sigma_{\max}\mu^{2}r}{\sqrt{n_{1}n_{2}}}
,
\epsilon\leq\lambda_{1}\left(1-\rho_{\min}\right)
, and
1-\frac{\epsilon}{\lambda_{1}}>\rho\geq\rho_{\min}
:

1.

\text{supp}\left(\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),t}\right)\subset\text{supp}\left({\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}\right)
for every
i\in[N]
.

2.

\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),t}-{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2\lambda_{t}\leq 4\sigma_{\max}\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}
for every
i\in[N]
.

3.

\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{L}}^{\epsilon}_{(i),t}-{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\epsilon+\rho\lambda_{t}
for every
i\in[N]
.

Moreover, we have

\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\epsilon}{}_{g,t}\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\epsilon}{}_{(i),g,t}^{T}-{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{V}^{\star}}_{(i),g}^{T}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}\left(\rho^{t}+\frac{\epsilon}{1-\rho}\right),\quad\text{for every }i\in[N].

(73)

and

\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{U}}^{\epsilon}{}_{(i),l,t}\hat{\mathbf{V}}^{\epsilon}{}_{(i),l,t}^{T}-{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{(i),l}{\mathbf{V}^{\star}}_{(i),l}^{T}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}\left(\rho^{t}+\frac{\epsilon}{1-\rho}\right),\quad\text{for every }i\in[N].

(74)

Remark

The definition of the term
\rho_{\min}
in the statement of the above theorem is kept intentionally implicit to streamline the presentation. In what follows, we will give an estimate of the requirements on
\alpha
purely in terms of the parameters of the problem. Lemma
14
requires
\alpha=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\theta}{\mu^{4}r^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}}\right)^{8}\right)
. Lemma
15
requires
\alpha=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\mu^{2}r}\right)
. Lemma
16
requires
\alpha=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\theta}{\mu^{4}r^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\sigma_{\max}}\right)^{12}\right)
. Lemma
17
requires
\alpha=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\theta}{\mu^{4}r^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\sigma_{\max}}\right)^{12}\right)
. Lemma
18
requires
\alpha=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\theta}{\mu^{2}r}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\sigma_{\max}}\right)^{6}\right)
. And Lemma
19
requires
\alpha=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\theta}{\mu^{2}r}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\sigma_{\max}}\right)^{2}\right)
. As
C_{4}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\theta}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\max}}{\sigma_{\min}}\right)^{10}\right)
, the additional requirement in Theorem
21
requires
\alpha=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\theta}{\mu^{4}r^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\sigma_{\max}}\right)^{20}\right)
. Taking the intersections of all these requirements, we can derive the upper bound on
\alpha
as
\alpha=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\theta}{\mu^{4}r^{2}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\min}}{\sigma_{\max}}\right)^{20}\right)
.

Proof.

We will prove this theorem by induction.

Base case:

At
t=1
,
\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{(i),0}=0
. As
\lambda_{1}=\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}\sigma_{\max}
, we have
\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),1}=\text{Hard}_{\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}\sigma_{\max}}\left[\mathbf{M}_{(i)}\right]
. By definition of hard-thresholding, if the
jk
-th entry of
\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),1}
is nonzero, we know
\left|[\mathbf{M}_{(i)}]_{jk}\right|>\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}\sigma_{\max}
. Since
\left|[{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}\right|\leq\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}\sigma_{\max}
for each
j
and
k
, we must have
\left|[{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}\right|>0
. This proves Claim
1
for
t=1
.

Now we will prove Claim
2
holds when
t=1
. If
[\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),1}]_{jk}=0
, we know
\left|[{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}+[{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}\right|\leq\mu^{2}r/{\overline{n}}\sigma_{\max}
, thus
\left|[{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}\right|\leq 2\mu^{2}r/{\overline{n}}\sigma_{\max}
. If
[\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),1}]_{jk}\neq 0
, by the definition of hard-thresholding, we know
[\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),1}]_{jk}=[\mathbf{M}_{(i)}]_{jk}=[{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}+[{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}
. By rearranging terms, we have
\left|[{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}-[\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),1}]_{jk}\right|=\left|[{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}\right|\leq\mu^{2}r/{\overline{n}}\sigma_{\max}
. We hence proved Claim
2
for
t=1
.

Since
\mathbf{E}_{(i),1}={\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}-\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),1}
, we have
\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i),1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}\sigma_{\max}
for each
i
as well. Also, by Claim
1
,
\mathbf{E}_{(i),1}
’s are
\alpha
-sparse. Therefore by Lemma
20
, when
\alpha\leq\frac{\rho_{\min}^{2}}{4\mu^{4}r^{2}C_{4}^{2}}\leq\frac{\rho^{2}}{4\mu^{4}r^{2}C_{4}^{2}}
,
\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{(i),1}-{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\frac{\mu^{2}r}{{\overline{n}}}\sigma_{\max}C_{4}\leq\rho\lambda_{1}
. From the definition of
\epsilon
-optimality and triangle inequality, we know
\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{L}}^{\epsilon}_{(i),1}-{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\rho\lambda_{1}+\epsilon
. We thus proved Claim
3
for
t=1
.

Induction step:

Now supposing that Claims
1
,
2
, and
3
hold for iterations
1,\cdots,t
, we will show their correctness for the iteration
t+1
.
Since Claim
3
holds for iteration
t
, we know
\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{L}}^{\epsilon}_{(i),t}-{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\rho\lambda_{t}+\epsilon
under the condition
\alpha\leq\frac{\rho^{2}}{4\mu^{4}r^{2}C_{4}^{2}}
. With the choice of
\lambda_{t+1}=\rho\lambda_{t}+\epsilon
, if the
jk
-th entry of
\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),t+1}
is nonzero, we have
\left|[{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}+[{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}-[\hat{\mathbf{L}}^{\epsilon}_{(i),t}]_{jk}\right|>\lambda_{t+1}
. Since
\left|[{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}-[\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{(i),t}]_{jk}\right|\leq\lambda_{t+1}
, we must have
\left|[{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}\right|>0
. This proves Claim
1
for iteration
t+1
.

We will now proceed to prove Claim
2
. We consider each entry of
\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),t+1}=\text{Hard}_{\lambda_{t+1}}\left[{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}-\hat{\mathbf{L}}^{\epsilon}_{(i),t}\right]
. From the definition of hard-thresholding, we know
\left|[\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),t+1}]_{jk}-\left([{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}+[{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}-[\hat{\mathbf{L}}^{\epsilon}_{(i),t}]_{jk}\right)\right|\leq\lambda_{t+1}
. Remember that we know
\left|[{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}-[\hat{\mathbf{L}}^{\epsilon}_{(i),t}]_{jk}\right|\leq\lambda_{t+1}
from the correctness of Claim
3
at iteration
t
and the upper bound on
\alpha
, we can derive
\left|[\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),t+1}]_{jk}-[{\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}]_{jk}\right|\leq 2\lambda_{t+1}
by triangle inequality. We hence prove Claim
2
.

For Claim
3
, since
\mathbf{E}_{(i),t+1}={\mathbf{S}^{\star}}_{(i)}-\hat{\mathbf{S}}_{(i),t+1}
, we have
\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i),t+1}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2\lambda_{t+1}
for each
i
as well. Also, by Claim
1
at iteration
t
,
\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}
’s are
\alpha
-sparse at iteration
t
. Therefore by Lemma
20
,
\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{(i),t+1}-{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2\sqrt{\alpha}\mu^{2}rC_{4}\lambda_{t+1}
. Under the constraint that
\alpha\leq\frac{\rho^{2}}{4\mu^{4}r^{2}C_{4}^{2}}
, we know
\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{L}}_{(i),t+1}-{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\rho\lambda_{t+1}
. From the definition of
\epsilon
-optimality and triangle inequality, we have
\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{L}}^{\epsilon}_{(i),t+1}-{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\rho\lambda_{t+1}+\epsilon
. We thus proved Claim
3
at iteration
t+1
.

Combining them, we can conclude that
1
,
2
, and
3
hold for every
t=1,2,\cdots
.

Finally, we will prove (
73
) and (
74
). We have known that
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g,t}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g,t}^{T}=\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i)}
, then from similar analysis of (
), we have,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g,t}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g,t}^{T}-{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{V}^{\star}}_{(i),g}^{T}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}\left({\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}+{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}+\mathbf{E}_{(i)}\right)-{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{P}_{{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}}{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),l}\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big|\left(\mathbf{T}_{(0)}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{-2}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{T}_{(0)}+\mathbf{T}_{(0)}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{-1}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}^{T}+\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{-1}{\hat{\mathbf{H}}}{}_{g}^{T}\mathbf{T}_{(0)}+\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}\delta\mathbf{H}_{g}^{T}\right)

\displaystyle({\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}+\mathbf{E}_{(i),t})-{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}_{(i),g}\Big|\Big|_{\infty}.

In Lemma
20
, we have shown that each term above is upper bounded by
\mathcal{O}(\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{\infty})
. Therefore by Claim
2
, we have
\left\lVert\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{g,t}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{(i),g,t}^{T}-{\mathbf{U}^{\star}}_{g}{\mathbf{V}^{\star}}_{(i),g}^{T}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}_{(i),t}\right\rVert_{\infty})=\mathcal{O}(\lambda_{t})=\mathcal{O}(\rho^{t}+\frac{\epsilon}{1-\rho})
. (
73
) follows accordingly by triangle inequality.

We can prove (
74
) in a similar way. This completes our proof of Theorem
21
.
∎

Appendix D

Auxiliary Lemma

This section discusses some helper lemmas useful for our main proofs. These lemmas are mostly derived from basic linear algebra and series.

The following lemma is a well-known result and provides an upper bound on the norm of product matrices.

Lemma 22
.

Form two matrices
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
and
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
, we have,

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{F}

and

\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}\right\rVert_{2}

Proof.

The proof is straightforward and can be found in
Sun and Luo (2016)
.
∎

Lemma 23
.

For
x,y\in[0,1)
such that
x+y<1
, the following relation holds:

\displaystyle\sum_{p_{1}+p_{2}\geq 1}x^{p_{1}+p_{2}}\leq\frac{2x}{1-x}.

(75a)

Proof.

This proof follows from the direct calculation.

\displaystyle\sum_{p_{1}+p_{2}\geq 1}x^{p_{1}+p_{2}}=\sum_{p_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{p_{2}=0}^{\infty}x^{p_{1}+p_{2}}-1

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{p_{1}=0}^{\infty}x^{p_{1}}\right)\left(\sum_{p_{2}=0}^{\infty}x^{p_{2}}\right)-1=\frac{1}{(1-x)^{2}}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{2x-x^{2}}{(1-x)^{2}}\leq\frac{2x}{1-x}.

∎

We also present a lemma related to the Schur complement of block matrices.

Lemma 24
.

For symmetric matrices
\mathbf{A}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{0}\times r_{0}},\mathbf{A}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{1}},\cdots,\mathbf{A}_{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{1}}
, and
\mathbf{B}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{0}\times r_{N}}
for
i\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
, we can construct a symmetric block matrix
\mathbf{C}
as,

\mathbf{C}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{A}_{0}&\mathbf{B}_{1}&\mathbf{B}_{2}&\cdots&\mathbf{B}_{N}\\
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{T}&\mathbf{A}_{1}&0&\cdots&0\\
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{T}&0&\mathbf{A}_{2}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\cdots&0\\
\mathbf{B}_{N}^{T}&0&0&\cdots&\mathbf{A}_{N}\end{pmatrix}.

(76)

Then,
\mathbf{C}
is positive definite if and only if
\mathbf{A}_{1},\mathbf{A}_{2},\cdots,\mathbf{A}_{N}
are positive definite and
\mathbf{A}_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{i}^{T}
is positive definite.

Proof.

Sicne
\mathbf{A}_{i}
’s are positive definite, they are invertible. Thus we can decompose
\mathbf{C}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}=\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{I}&\mathbf{B}_{1}\mathbf{A}_{1}^{-1}&\mathbf{B}_{2}\mathbf{A}_{2}^{-1}&\cdots&\mathbf{B}_{N}\mathbf{A}_{N}^{-1}\\
0&\mathbf{I}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&0&\mathbf{I}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&0\\
0&0&0&\cdots&\mathbf{I}\end{pmatrix}}_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{A}_{0}-\sum_{i}\mathbf{B}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{i}^{T}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&\mathbf{A}_{1}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&0\\
0&0&\cdots&\mathbf{A}_{N}\end{pmatrix}}_{\mathbf{C}_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\underbrace{\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{I}&0&0&\cdots&0\\
\mathbf{A}_{1}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{1}^{T}&\mathbf{I}&0&\cdots&0\\
\mathbf{A}_{2}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{2}^{T}&0&\mathbf{I}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&0\\
\mathbf{A}_{N}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{N}^{T}&0&0&\cdots&\mathbf{I}\end{pmatrix}}_{\mathbf{C}_{1}^{T}}.

On the right hand side,
\mathbf{C}_{1}
and
\mathbf{C}_{1}^{T}
are both invertible. Thus,
\mathbf{C}
is positive definite if and only if
\mathbf{C}_{2}
is positive definite. Since
\mathbf{C}_{2}
is a block diagonal matrix, we prove the statement in the lemma.
∎

The following lemma provides an eigenvalue lower bound on the product of three matrices.

Lemma 25
.

For matrix
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, and symmetric positive semidefinite matrix
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, we know that,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}\right)\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{A}\right).

Proof.

The proof follows from the Courant–Fischer–Weyl variational principle.

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}\right)=\min_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\mathbf{v}^{T}\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)\min_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}\mathbf{v}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{B}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{A}\right).

∎

We finally present the lemma that provides an upper bound of the operator norm of block matrices.

Lemma 26
.

For a symmetric block matrix
\mathbf{C}
defined as

\mathbf{C}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{A}_{1}&\mathbf{B}_{12}&\mathbf{B}_{13}&\cdots&\mathbf{B}_{1N}\\
\mathbf{B}_{12}^{T}&\mathbf{A}_{2}&\mathbf{B}_{23}&\cdots&\mathbf{B}_{2N}\\
\mathbf{B}_{13}^{T}&\mathbf{B}_{23}^{T}&\mathbf{A}_{3}&\cdots&\mathbf{B}_{3N}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mathbf{B}_{1N}^{T}&\mathbf{B}_{2N}^{T}&\mathbf{B}_{3N}^{T}&\cdots&\mathbf{A}_{N}\end{pmatrix},

(77)

where
\mathbf{A}_{i}
’s are symmetric, we have,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{C}\right\rVert\leq\max_{i=1,\cdots,N}\{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert\}+\sqrt{2\sum_{i<j}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{ij}\right\rVert^{2}}.

(78)

Note: in (
78
), the diagonal blocks and off-diagonal blocks are treated differently.

Proof.

We first prove for the special case where
\mathbf{B}_{ij}=0
. In this case,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{C}\right\rVert^{2}=\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert\mathbf{C}\mathbf{v}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i=1,\cdots,N}\{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\}\times\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\mathbf{v}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}=\max_{i=1,\cdots,N}\{\left\lVert\mathbf{A}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\}.

We then prove for the special case where
\mathbf{A}_{i}=0
. We have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{C}\right\rVert^{2}=\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert\mathbf{C}\mathbf{v}\right\rVert^{2}=\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\lVert\sum_{j\neq i}\mathbf{B}_{ij}\mathbf{v}_{j}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j,k\neq i}\mathbf{v}_{k}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{ik}^{T}\mathbf{B}_{ij}\mathbf{v}_{j}\leq\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j,k\neq i}\left\lVert\mathbf{v}_{k}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{ik}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{ij}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{v}_{j}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{ij}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbf{v}_{j}\right\rVert\right)^{2}\leq\max_{\left\lVert\mathbf{v}\right\rVert=1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{ij}\right\rVert^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{j}\left\lVert\mathbf{v}_{j}\right\rVert^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i<j}\left\lVert\mathbf{B}_{ij}\right\rVert^{2},

where we used Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the second inequality.

By applying triangle inequality of the matrix operator norm, we can combine the upper bounds derived from two special cases and obtain (
78
).
∎
